# Changelog

All notable changes to YesMem are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- Keep helper scripts in the project, not in /tmp
- Read a project's rules from the checkout that contains the session
- Number a project's own rules from a reserved range

### Fixed

- Install the standard rules on update, not only on setup
- Read a missing node_modules as an environment gap
- Read an exact flag that is also a prefix of a dangerous one

### Documentation

- Tell a fresh installation that a project may number its rules
- Mirror the installed master and file the helper-script rule
- The recorder fix and the compare link
- A release tags a green commit and carries real notes

## [2.5.1] - 2026-11-10

### Added

- Gate deletions deterministically with an approval stage
- Write the master rules once and seed a numberless project overlay
- Compose the master RULES.md with a project's numberless deviations
- The pause whitelist holds the line, the docs follow
- Nudge instead of stopping an agent that is still working
- Skillcheck informs the orchestrator instead of pausing the agent
- The sandbox knows the tools a profile requires and keeps its cache
- The verification becomes Phase 6, FINISH moves to Phase 7
- Deliver a DONE payload only on a fresh verdict for its report
- The run judges the working tree, not only the commits
- The project profile admits its own test and coverage command
- Verify_claims takes the report session
- Decide DONE from the pre-check's verdict
- Name the branch a verdict belongs to
- Report the failing lines of an init proof run
- Add --init to verify-claims and a Phase 1 profile step
- Detect a project stack and prove it in the sandbox
- Name the missing verify profile in the block message
- Read the project profile from .yesmem/verify.yaml
- Escalate a report after three blocked rounds instead of asking a fourth time

### Changed

- ParseRm keeps no second return
- The plan lookup reports found explicitly
- Close the skillcheck round on the notice so the next window re-arms
- The project profile declares its toolchain bridge
- The project's own test profile, narrow enough for the sandbox
- Deterministic profile validation order and clearer init docs

### Fixed

- Judge masked roots and their ancestors in both path forms
- Serialise the done gate per agent so a late block cannot follow an escalation
- Mask through symlinked roots, and skip what this host cannot sandbox
- A silent exit is an environment gap, and the worktree test checks its precondition
- Read quoted rules as text unless an interpreter runs them
- Read the payload of a shell and keep the approval per line
- Narrow the approval and bound the deletion gate's verdict
- Close the deletion gate's indirect paths
- Allow a full project suite ten more minutes of machine time
- Contain the deviation block and bound what it can do
- Ground a citation only in the standard catalogue
- Keep a numbered overlay line out of the catalogue
- Keep the detected project types in a fixed order
- The plan claim speaks about its caller, and a read failure ends the walk
- The escalated case may not silence the idle layer for good
- Close the findings of the cold, consequence and security review
- The plan lookup follows the caller's order and survives one unreadable thread
- A nudge does not settle the idle layer, and the notices are sanitized
- A plan claim is judged against the agent's own threads
- The verifier's own cache base is trusted below the home
- The writable cache stays inside the verifier's own root
- The hold outlives another guard's pause, and the watchdog tells the truth
- A held DONE keeps its clock and never goes silent
- The pending hold defers the payload too, and the retry keeps asking for it
- One round per decision, an owner-checked release, and no client-side off switch
- Refuse a profile fragment, and let the reported command match the one that ran
- The command right comes from the committed blob, and untracked work cannot be hidden by local git config
- Bare repos are a no-op, the command right comes from the commit, the read stays read-only
- A command right needs a committed profile; exit code stops counting the criterion
- The limit message names the wait that expired
- Announce the hold once, and file a limit when the slot never frees
- Harden the verdict path against the review findings
- Diagnose a stale verdict from the verdict itself
- Quote the printed command and bound the gate's memory
- Derive the diff base and bound the stored finding
- Derive the report session and plan thread from the section
- One decision per tick, and a failed pre-check records its error
- Refuse a toolchain cache in a credentials directory and other accounts' homes
- Refuse a symlinked profile and a language without test-file patterns
- Refuse composed home mounts and ancestors of masked roots
- Only a missing profile earns the init hint; reject report flags in init
- Refuse an unsandboxed proof and name the clean clone
- Bound the profile read, refuse empty lists and a symlinked parent
- Refuse profile mounts and loader variables the sandbox masks
- Hand the merged project profile to the engine context
- Count blocked rounds per attempt, not per report text
- Deliver like the relay does, hold unconditionally, sign the whole verdict
- Keep the cause in the verdict signature, owe the agent its message, bound the retries
- Hold on the gate's own prefix, retry a lost instruction, sign the verdict not its wording
- One fixed prompt for the hold, notices only on a change

### Documentation

- V2.5.1
- State the master plus overlay split for project rules
- Align the acceptance line with the loop contract, restore the phase-1 rule, keep triggers dot-free
- Name the campaign tools, keep the pin provenance in the pointer
- Bugfix campaign procedure as bundled skill, doc becomes a pointer
- Move 28 pins into RULES.md, skill sections and documents
- Name the skillcheck round in the notice and log the round close
- The skillcheck never pauses an agent
- The relay texts say seven, the docs guard follows the contract
- The verification is Phase 6, the handoff Phase 7
- The DONE payload is decided before it is delivered
- The command needs no ids, and the plan comes first
- The plan comes before the pre-check, and the indexer brake is gone
- The DONE gate reads a verdict instead of running a check
- Nenne die geduldeten Anspruchstypen, nicht Klassen
- Praezisiere die Politik, Merge-Toleranz und tests_exit-Ausnahme
- Unterscheide --policy yesloop-done von leerer Policy
- Name the lock wait, the unsignable numbers and the scope of the hold

### Testing

- Wait for the recorder before judging the last message
- Let the sandbox check name the failure it sees
- Pin the full trigger set and the deliberate absence of the yesloop trigger
- The campaign skill survives catalogue discovery, not just its file
- Gofmt the plan-thread table
- A repeated refusal is one round, not one per message

## [2.5.0] - 2026-11-03

### Added

- A third level, and the block stops capping rows
- The go profile binds the module cache so a suite can compile
- A language profile declares the cache mounts its sandbox needs
- Report session, plan thread, a guarded budget and a capped report
- Install RULES.md into the data dir, where the guard reads it
- Verify-claims judges a report through the daemon
- The opencode guard asks the daemon instead of holding its own model
- The nudge asks the same core, and a cited rule must exist
- Block a report without a claim before any run
- The decision names every blocking reason of a class
- The block message carries its own rules per verdict class
- Mutation and coverage_delta claims, language-agnostic through the profile
- The DONE gate verifies instead of believing
- Verify_claims as an operation, with the gate as one caller among others
- Two yesmem claim types, one evidence rule for both callers
- Decide a report by its classes, not by its exit code
- Preprocess the rules, read the block set from the policy, cap the context
- Add the proofboy verifier core (M1-M3)
- Report the rule guard's model and endpoint, or that it is inactive
- Resolve the Claude Code guard model through one shared resolver
- Speak the Anthropic messages format and resolve the model at runtime

### Changed

- The plan block's JSX runtime follows OpenTUI 0.8
- Drop a dead state copy, document the per-reason bound
- Read mutants before the baseline run, confine the coverage command
- Judge in one core and serve it as guard_check

### Fixed

- Show the ancestry evidence with the pins it ran with
- Pin every scratch command and classify the probe's causes too
- Keep the partial clone offline and split the 124 causes
- Fetch branch_pushed with a blob filter and refute a missing ref
- The template declares the done_verify section it ships
- The profile cannot disarm the interpreter guards or leak the home
- The profile may not mount a relative path or own the run's PATH
- The sandbox bridge keeps its guarantees under review
- The installed catalogue carries the blockable rule numbers
- A queued or released verdict outlives a read error
- The environment block arms its own retry lever
- One sanitiser for both harnesses, and the read-only list takes glued flags
- The nudge keeps its no-match, and a notice is not a ghost skill
- The status line tells a missing section from a claimless one
- The cache key covers the whole call, and a write is judged on its content
- Hold the presence block, bound the reason line
- The finding status goes through the sanitiser as well
- The check line says it is the report's own text
- Keep the findings of a machine verdict, and state the wait the worker enforces
- A failed run is not sent to the report, and a limit names the bound that expired
- No unsafe rewrite of a symlink, no repo-configured diff driver, bounded coverage reads
- Close the cold-review findings on allowance, coverage and output bounds
- Resolve module-prefixed coverage paths, judge instrumented lines only
- The second review round, base resolution and re-verify
- The review findings, from the two traps to the silent holes
- Keep the evidence adapter harness-neutral and never revive a dead row
- Resolve programs and bubblewrap against the run's PATH, not the caller's
- Read the installed rules from the installation directory
- Enforce only what the installed policy allows, and check patterns before credentials
- Make an honest compiled report verifiable and bound the run
- Close the review findings on the core
- Close the false-confirmation and escape findings of the cold review
- Do not send a pinned model to an endpoint that never resolved it
- Keep the account credential out of a redirect

### Reverted

- Keep the plugin unchanged, the adapter is its own loop

### Documentation

- The plan mirrors the six phases and ticks per step
- A plan without items draws no sidebar block
- The installed RULES.md, its precedence, and the verify-claims flags
- The three end-to-end cases against the running daemon
- The reason line is bounded, the block is held
- The README describes the adapter, not the removed guard internals
- The plugin is an adapter, and the guard doc says so
- The report without a claim is blocked, not queued
- The presence block and the reason list
- Complete the regeneration for the review-fix commits
- An environment gap blames the environment, not a machine
- Regenerate for the four merges of today and the v2.4.2 tag
- The verifier and the guard, and the report shape the gate now prints
- The report block keeps the shape, the gate carries the rules
- A test charter for scope, determinism and proportionality
- Regenerate CHANGELOG

### Testing

- The head-check fixture ignores the host git config
- Pin the resolution pin, the keyless provider and the answer keys
- The flag set writes to io.Discard, not to an unclosed file
- Cover the provider branch of the model resolution
- The installed module list no longer carries http.ts
- The gate end to end, with a faked and an honest report
- Point every test home at its XDG roots, not just HOME
